Proof.

By the Milnor-Švarc lemma, we can find constants
\tau_{x},\kappa_{x}>0
such that

\frac{1}{\tau_{x}}\cdot l(\gamma)+\kappa_{x}>d(x,\gamma y)\geq\tau_{x}\cdot l(\gamma)-\kappa_{x}.

Since
F
is compact, we can take
\tau=\min_{x\in F}\{\tau_{x}\}
and
\kappa=\max_{x\in F}\{\kappa_{x}\}
.
∎

Definition 3.13
.

The space
\mathcal{A}^{\rm exp}_{\Gamma}(M)
of
\Gamma
-equivariant exponentially rapidly decreasing smoothing kernels consists of the following functions:

\{k\in C^{\infty}(M\times M)^{\Gamma}:\forall q\in\mathbb{N},\ {\text{t}here\ exists}\ A_{q}>0\ {\text{s}uch\ that}\ \sup_{x,y\in M}|e^{qd(x,y)}\nabla^{m}_{x}\nabla^{n}_{y}k(x,y)|<A_{q},\ \forall m,n\in\mathbb{N}\}.

It is proved in
[
PP2
, Proposition A.2]
that the
\Gamma
-equivariant smoothing operators of exponentially rapidly decay form an algebra that we denote by

\mathcal{A}^{{\rm exp}}_{\Gamma}(M)
. Moreover, for each fixed
t
the operators appearing in the Connes-Moscovici projection

e^{-tD^{2}},\quad\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\sqrt{t}D,\quad e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\sqrt{t}D.

are in fact in
\mathcal{A}^{{\rm exp}}_{\Gamma}(M)
.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
F
be a compact subset of
M
. We can find a constant
C
large enough so that

\int_{F\times M^{k}}e^{-C\left(d(x,y_{1})+\ldots d(x,y_{k})\right)}\;dxdy_{1}\ldots dy_{k}

is convergent.

Proof.

Because the
\Gamma
action on
M
is proper and cocompact, there is an open subset
\mathcal{F}
of
M
such that the closure
\overline{\mathcal{F}}
is a compact subset of
M
and

\bigcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma\mathcal{F}=M.

By Lemma
3.12

\int_{F\times\mathcal{F}^{k}}e^{-C\left(d(x,y_{1})+\ldots d(x,y_{k})\right)}\;dxdy_{1}\ldots dy_{k}\leq\sum_{\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma}\int_{F\times\mathcal{F}^{k}}e^{-C\cdot\left(d(x,\gamma_{1}y_{1})+\ldots d(x,\gamma_{k}y_{k})\right)}\;dxdy_{1}\ldots dy_{k}\\
\leq\sum_{\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma}\int_{F\times\mathcal{F}^{k}}e^{-C\left(\sum_{i}\tau_{i}\cdot l(\gamma_{i})-\kappa_{i}\right)}\;dxdy_{1}\ldots dy_{k}\\
\leq C_{N}+\vol(F\times\mathcal{F}^{k})\sum_{\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma,l(\gamma_{i})>N}e^{-C\left(\sum_{i}\tau_{i}\cdot l(\gamma_{i})-\kappa_{i}\right)}

Because
\Gamma
is finitely generated, there are constants
C_{\Gamma}
,
K_{\Gamma}
satisfying

\#\left\{\gamma\in\Gamma,l(\gamma)\leq k\right\}\leq C_{\Gamma}\cdot e^{K_{\Gamma}k}.

Thus,

\sum_{\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma,l(\gamma_{i})>N}e^{-C\left(\sum_{i}\tau_{i}\cdot l(\gamma_{i})-\kappa_{i}\right)}\leq\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}C_{\Gamma}\cdot e^{\sum_{i}\left((K_{\Gamma}-C\tau_{i})k_{i}+C\kappa_{i}\right)}

We first choose
C
large enough so that

K_{\Gamma}-C\tau_{i}<0.

If we further choose
N
so that

N\cdot\sum_{i}\tau_{i}>C\sum_{i}\kappa_{i},

then the above summation is finite. ∎

Proposition 3.15
.

If
\psi\in\mathsf{E}_{\rm AS}^{k}(M,\gamma)
is EG then
\rho(\psi)
is a cyclic cochain on
\mathcal{A}^{\rm exp}_{\Gamma}(M)
.

Proof.

Since
\rho(\psi)
is a cyclic cochain on
\mathcal{A}_{\Gamma}^{c}(M)
, it suffices to show that if
\psi
is EG, then

\rho(\psi)\left(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k}\right)

is convergent for any
A_{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{\rm exp}_{\Gamma}(M)
. By the inequalities in Definition
3.4
,

|\rho(\psi)\left(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k}\right)(y_{0},\dots,y_{k})|=\left|\sum_{\eta\in\Gamma}\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}(\eta)\cdot\psi\left(\eta,y_{0},\cdots,y_{k}\right)A_{0}(y_{0},y_{1})\ldots A_{k}(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})\right|\\
\leq\left|A_{\psi}\cdot e^{K_{\psi,0}\cdot d(y_{0},y_{1})+\cdots+K_{\psi,k-1}\cdot d(y_{k-1},y_{k})+K_{\psi,k}\cdot d(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})}\cdot A_{0}(y_{0},y_{1})\ldots A_{k}(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})\right|.\\

Since
A_{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{\rm exp}_{\Gamma}(M)
, we can find constants
q
so that for all
0\leq i\leq k

\left|A_{i}(x,y)\right|\leq A_{q}\cdot e^{-qd(x,y)}.

It follows that

|\rho(\psi)\left(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k}\right)(y_{0},\dots,y_{k})|\\
\leq\left|A_{\psi}A_{q}\cdot e^{K_{\psi,0}\cdot d(y_{0},y_{1})+\cdots+K_{\psi,k-1}\cdot d(y_{k-1},y_{k})+K_{\psi,k}\cdot d(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})}\cdot e^{-q\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}d(y_{i},y_{i+1})-qd(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})}\right|\\
=\left|A_{\psi}A_{q}\cdot e^{\left(K_{\psi,0}-q\right)\cdot d(y_{0},y_{1})+\cdots+\left(K_{\psi,k-1}-q\right)\cdot d(y_{k-1},y_{k})+\left(K_{\psi,k}-q\right)\cdot d(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})}\right|.

Since we can choose
q
arbitrarily large and
\psi
has compact support in
y_{0}
, it follows from Lemma
3.14
that

\rho(\psi)\left(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k}\right)

is convergent.
∎

3.3.
Monomial approximation

Let us take
c\in\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
which is EG and consider

(3.16)

\displaystyle\Phi\circ\tau(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k})=\rho\circ\Psi(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k}),

where
A_{i}(x,y,t)
are the smoothing kernels appearing in the Connes-Moscovici projection, that is

(3.17)

e^{-tD^{2}},\quad\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\sqrt{t}D,\quad e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\sqrt{t}D.

In this case, by
[
wang-wang-jncg
, Theorem 6.15]
, we can find constants
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},\eta_{i}>0
:

(3.18)

\displaystyle\Big|A_{i}(x,y,t)\Big|\leq\alpha_{i}\cdot t^{-\beta_{i}}\cdot e^{-\eta_{i}\cdot\frac{d(x,y)}{t}}.

Later in the article we shall abstract this property into the definition
of a family of operators
\{Q(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
with
exponential control
, see
Definition
4.25
.

Definition 3.19
.

Let us introduce the index

(3.20)

\displaystyle I=(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{k})\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}

and put

(3.21)

\displaystyle|I|_{\gamma}=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}l(\gamma_{i}^{-1}\gamma_{i+1})+l(\gamma_{k}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0}).

By definition,

(3.22)

\displaystyle\left|(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{k})\right|_{\gamma}=\left|(\eta\gamma_{0},\ldots,\eta\gamma_{k})\right|_{\gamma},\quad\eta\in Z_{\gamma}.

Thus, for any
n>0
, the set

(3.23)

\displaystyle\{I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}:|I|_{\gamma}\leq n\}

might not be finite. However, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 3.24
.

We can find constants
C_{\gamma},K_{\gamma}>0
such that

(3.25)

\#\{I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}:|I|_{\gamma}\leq n,\gamma_{0}\in\supp\left(\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}\right)\}\leqslant C_{\gamma}\cdot e^{K_{\gamma}\cdot n}

Proof.

By the triangle inequality,

(3.26)

\displaystyle l(\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\leq l(\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma_{1})+\ldots+l(\gamma_{k-1}^{-1}\gamma_{k})+l(\gamma_{k}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})=|I|_{\gamma}\leq n.

Since the group
\Gamma
is finitely generated, there exist constants
C_{\Gamma},K_{\Gamma}>0
such that

(3.27)

\displaystyle\#\left\{Z_{\gamma}\gamma_{0}\in Z_{\gamma}\backslash\Gamma|l(\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\leq n\right\}=\#\left\{\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma|l(\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\leq n\right\}\leqslant C_{\Gamma}e^{K_{\Gamma}\cdot n}.

Because the cut-off function
\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}
has compact support in each
Z_{\gamma}
-orbit in
\Gamma
, we assume that
\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}
is supported on one element in each
Z_{\gamma}
-orbit. Then

(3.28)

\displaystyle\#\left\{\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma|l(\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\leq n,\gamma_{0}\in\supp\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}\right\}\leq C_{\Gamma}e^{K_{\Gamma}\cdot n}.

On the other hand,

(3.29)

\displaystyle l(\gamma_{0}\gamma_{i}^{-1})\leq l(\gamma_{0}\gamma_{1}^{-1})+\ldots+l(\gamma_{i-1}\gamma_{i}^{-1})\leq|I|_{\gamma}\leq n,

we have that

(3.30)

\displaystyle\#\{\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma:|I|_{\gamma}\leq n,\gamma_{0}\in\supp\left(\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}\right)\}\leqslant\left(C_{\Gamma}e^{K_{\Gamma}\cdot n}\right)^{2},\quad 1\leq i\leq k.

Hence, we can choose

(3.31)

\displaystyle C_{\gamma}=C_{\Gamma}^{2k+1}

\displaystyle K_{\gamma}=(2k+1)K_{\Gamma}.

∎

We define

(3.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t):=\left(c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{k})\cdot\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\chi(\gamma_{0}^{-1}y_{0})\cdots\chi\left(\gamma_{k}^{-1}y_{k}\right)\right)\cdot A_{0}(y_{0},y_{1},t)\cdots A_{k}(y_{k},\gamma y_{0},t)

Below are some immediate facts about
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}
:

(1)

we have that

(3.33)

\displaystyle\rho\circ\Psi(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k})=\int_{M^{k+1}}\left(\sum_{I}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\right)dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k};

(2)

for each
I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}
, the monoids
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}
is compactly supported on
M^{\times(k+1)}
and

(3.34)

\displaystyle\vol\left(\supp\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}\right)\leq\left(\vol(\supp\chi)\right)^{k+1}.

Lemma 3.35
.

If
c\in\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
is EG, there exists constants
n_{0},t_{0}>0
and
\alpha,\beta,\eta>0
such that,

(3.36)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)|\leq\alpha\cdot t^{-\beta}\cdot e^{-\frac{\eta\cdot|I|_{\gamma}}{t}},\quad y_{i}\in M.

for any
|I|_{\gamma}>n_{0}
and
0<t<t_{0}
.

Proof.

Because
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}
is supported on

(3.37)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{-1}y_{i}\in\supp\chi,

it follows from Lemma
3.12
that

(3.38)

\displaystyle d(y_{i},y_{i+1})>\tau_{i}l(\gamma^{-1}_{i}\gamma_{i+1})-\kappa_{i}

\displaystyle d(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})>\tau_{k}l(\gamma_{k}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})-\kappa_{k}.

Put

(3.39)

\displaystyle\tau=\min\{\tau_{i}\},

\displaystyle\kappa=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\kappa_{i}.

By the EG condition,

(3.40)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)|\leq A_{c}\cdot\exp\left(K_{c}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}l(\gamma_{i}^{-1}\gamma_{i+1})+l(\gamma_{k}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\right)\right)\left|A_{0}(y_{0},y_{1},t)\ldots A_{k}(y_{k},\gamma y_{0},t)\right|

Using Lemma
3.35
,

(3.41)

|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)|\leq A_{c}\cdot\exp\left(K_{c}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}l(\gamma_{i}^{-1}\gamma_{i+1})+l(\gamma_{k}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\right)\right)\\
\prod_{i=0}^{k}\alpha_{i}\cdot t^{-\sum_{i=0}^{k}\beta_{i}}\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\eta_{i}d(y_{i},y_{i+1})-d(y_{k},\gamma y_{0})}{t}\right)

Thus, we can find suitable constants
\alpha,\beta>0
so that

(3.42)

|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)|\leq\alpha\cdot t^{-\beta}\exp\left(\left(K_{c}-\frac{\tau}{t}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}l(\gamma_{i}^{-1}\gamma_{i+1})+l(\gamma_{k}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{0})\right)+\frac{\kappa}{t}\right)\\
=\alpha\cdot t^{-\beta}\exp\left(\left(K_{c}-\frac{\tau}{t}\right)\cdot|I|_{\gamma}+\frac{\kappa}{t}\right)

if we choose
t
small enough and
|I|_{\gamma}
large enough so that

(3.43)

\displaystyle K_{c}-\frac{\tau}{t}<0

\displaystyle\tau|I|_{\gamma}>\kappa.

This completes the proof with
n_{0}=\frac{\kappa}{\tau}
.
∎

Theorem 3.44
.

If
c\in\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{\gamma}(\Gamma)
is EG, then the integral

(3.45)

\displaystyle\int_{M^{k+1}}\left(\sum_{|I|_{\gamma}>n_{0}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\right)dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}

is absolutely convergent for sufficiently small
t
. Moreover, we can find
A,\delta>0

such that as
t\downarrow 0

(3.46)

\displaystyle\sum_{|I|_{\gamma}>n_{0}}\int_{M^{k+1}}\left|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\right|dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}\leq A\cdot t^{-\beta}e^{-\frac{\delta\cdot n_{0}}{t}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.35
and (
3.34
),

(3.47)

\displaystyle\sum_{|I|_{\gamma}>n_{0}}\int_{M^{k+1}}\left|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\right|dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}\leq\left(\vol(\supp\chi)\right)^{k+1}\alpha\cdot t^{-\beta}\cdot\sum_{|I|_{\gamma}>N}e^{-\frac{\eta\cdot|I|_{\gamma}}{t}}

Using Lemma
3.24
,

(3.48)

\displaystyle\left(\vol(\supp\chi)\right)^{k+1}\sum_{|I|_{\gamma}>n_{0}}e^{-\frac{C\cdot|I|_{\gamma}}{t}}\leq\left(\vol(\supp\chi)\right)^{k+1}\alpha\cdot C_{\gamma}\cdot t^{-\beta}\cdot\sum_{n=n_{0}}^{\infty}e^{\left(K_{\gamma}-\frac{\eta}{t}\right)n}

When
t
is sufficiently small, we obtained the estimate.
∎

To sum up, we have obtained the following results:

Corollary 3.49
.

As
t\downarrow 0
,

(1)

the integral

(3.50)

\displaystyle\rho\circ\Psi(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k})=\sum_{I}\int_{M^{k+1}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\;dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}

is absolutely convergent;

(2)

there exists a finite subset
\mathcal{I}\subset\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}
such that

(3.51)

\displaystyle\rho\circ\Psi(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k})=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{M^{k+1}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\;dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}+O(t^{\infty}).

(3)

for each
I\in\mathcal{I}
, the function

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{k})\cdot\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\chi(\gamma_{0}^{-1}y_{0})A_{0}(y_{0},y_{1},t)\ldots\chi\left(\gamma_{k}^{-1}y_{k}\right)A_{k}(y_{k},\gamma y_{0},t)

is compactly supported.

The asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)
with
I\in\mathcal{I}
will be explicitly computed in the next two sections.

3.4.
Anti-symmetrization

As a function on
M^{\times(k+1)}
, the following

(3.52)

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}}\left(c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{k})\cdot\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\chi(\gamma_{0}^{-1}y_{0})\cdots\chi\left(\gamma_{k}^{-1}y_{k}\right)\right)=\sum_{\gamma_{0}\in Z_{\gamma}\backslash\Gamma}\Psi(c)(\gamma_{0},y_{0},\ldots y_{k})

is anti-symmetric by Lemma
2.20
. For any
I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}
, put

(3.53)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}):=c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{k})\cdot\chi^{\Gamma}_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\chi(\gamma_{0}^{-1}y_{0})\cdots\chi\left(\gamma_{k}^{-1}y_{k}\right)

and its anti-symmetrization by

(3.54)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}):=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k+1}}\text{sign}(\sigma)\cdot\mathcal{C}_{I}(y_{\sigma(0)},\ldots,y_{\sigma(k)}).

Because the right side of (
3.52
) is anti-symmetric, we have that

(3.55)

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}}\mathcal{C}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots,y_{k})=(k+1)!\sum_{I\in\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}}\mathcal{C}_{I}(y_{0},\ldots,y_{k})

Accordingly, we define

(3.56)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t):=\mathcal{C}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots,y_{k})\cdot A_{0}(y_{0},y_{1},t)\cdots A_{k}(y_{k},\gamma y_{0},t)

To obtain an analog of Lemma
3.35
for
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I,a}
, we introduce

(3.57)

\displaystyle|I|_{\gamma,a}=\min_{\sigma\in S_{k+1}}\left\{\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}l(\gamma_{\sigma(i)}^{-1}\gamma_{\sigma(i+1)}+l(\gamma_{\sigma(k)}^{-1}\gamma\gamma_{\sigma(0)})\right\}.

By the triangle inequality, one can check that

(3.58)

\displaystyle\frac{|I|_{\gamma}}{(k+1)^{2}}\leq|I|_{\gamma,a}\leq|I|_{\gamma}.

Hence, Lemma
3.35
and Theorem
3.44
remain true for
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I,a}
. In particular, we obtain the following Lemma:

Lemma 3.59
.

As
t\downarrow 0
,

(1)

the integral

(3.60)

\displaystyle\rho\circ\Psi(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k})=\frac{1}{(k+1)!}\cdot\sum_{I}\int_{M^{k+1}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\;dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}

is absolutely convergent;

(2)

there exist a finite subset
\mathcal{I}\subset\Gamma^{\times(k+1)}

(3.61)

\displaystyle\rho\circ\Psi(c)(A_{0},\ldots,A_{k})=\frac{1}{(k+1)!}\cdot\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{M^{k+1}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)\;dy_{0}\ldots dy_{k}+O(t^{\infty}).

(3)

for each
I\in\mathcal{I}
, the function
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{I,a}(y_{0},\ldots y_{k},t)

is compactly supported.

4.
New results on Volterra pseudodifferential operators

This section and the Appendix are devoted to the theory of Volterra pseudodifferential operators. Most of the results presented in the Appendix are classic whereas the material presented in this section is new.
In particular, we present in this section
results about Getzler rescaling and commutators; this technical results will play a major role
in the proof of our higher index theorem.

Let
M
be a smooth compact manifold without boundary and let
D
be an
L^{2}
-invertible
Dirac-type operator acting on the sections of a bundle of Clifford modules
E
. Then it is well known that
D^{-1}\in\Psi^{-1}(M,E)
. One way to introduce the Volterra calculus is to consider the heat operator
\partial_{t}+D^{2}
. Acting on
C^{\infty}_{+}(M,E)
, the subspace of

C^{\infty}_{+}(M,E)
consisting of sections supported on
M\times(-\infty,c]
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
, this operator is invertible; its inverse
(\partial_{t}+D^{2})^{-1}
is a Volterra
pseudodifferential operator of order
(-2)
. Put it differently, in the same way that classic
pseudodifferential operators are a receptacle for the inverses of elliptic operators, the Volterra calculus is a receptacle for the inverse of the parabolic operator
\partial_{t}+D^{2}
.

We refer the reader to the Appendix for

•

an introduction to the basic concepts;

•

the definition of
\Gamma
-equivariant Volterra calculus;

•

results relative to Getzler rescaling in the context of the Volterra calculus.

In this Section we only concentrate
on results that are not available in the literature.

4.1.
Getzler order and simplification

In the definition of the index pairing we shall need to consider the composition of heat-type operators with
t
fixed. Such composition is of course different from the composition
of the corresponding Volterra operators within the Volterra calculus, where composition in the
t
-variable, by convolution, appears. Such a difference prevents us from directly applying the Getzler-Volterra calculus to the short-time asymptotics computation of the index pairing. To overcome this problem, we develop in this subsection the tools that allow us to move operators in the index pairing
and to bring them in a sort of normal form, a form to which we can apply the results
of Ponge and Wang. One computational complication is the following, already present in
[
Ponge-Wang-2
]
and
[
LYZ
]
: if
\gamma\in\Gamma

we can usually consider two coordinate systems near a point
x\in M^{\gamma}
; one is the normal coordinates system centered at
x
and the other is the coordinate system defined by tubular neighborhood coordinates for
M^{\gamma}
, involving the normal bundle to
M^{\gamma}
near
x
. While the usual Volterra calculus works more conveniently with the tubular neighborhood coordinates, the Volterra-Getzler calculus works better with normal coordinates. Thanks to the formula connecting symbols in different coordinates systems
we can relate the two approaches; while this is conceptually clear, it becomes computationally
rather involved, and this explains the length of this subsection.

Let
\sigma:\operatorname{Cliff}(TM)\rightarrow\Lambda^{\bullet}T_{\mathbb{C}}M
be the symbol map. We take
x\in M
. Let
Q_{i},i=1,\cdots,l
be Volterra operators. Moreover, let
\Psi
be a function supported in a small neighborhood of
x
. Put

\widetilde{J}(x,\gamma,t):=\int_{M^{\times(k-1)}}K_{Q_{1}}\left(x,x_{1},t\right)\Psi(x_{1})K_{Q_{2}}\left(x_{1},x_{2},t\right)\cdots\Psi(x_{k-1})K_{Q_{k}}\left(x_{k-1},\gamma x,t\right)dx_{1}\cdots dx_{k-1}

As in
[
Ponge-Wang-2
]
we fix tubular coordinates near
x_{0}\in M_{a}^{\gamma}
(where
M_{a}^{\gamma}
is a connected component of
M^{\gamma}
of dimension
a
); we write
x
in the coordinates associated with such a
\gamma
-invariant chart as
(y,w)\in\mathbb{R}^{a}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-a}
via
\phi_{\rm tub}(y,w)
, with
\phi_{\rm tub}
the inverse of the tubular coordinate chart.
Because
\Psi
is supported in a neighbourhood
U_{0}
of
x
corresponding to a neighbourhood in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we can write

\widetilde{J}(x,\gamma,t):=\int_{\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)^{\times(k-1)}}K_{Q_{1}}\left(x,\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}),t\right)\Psi(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}))\\
K_{Q_{2}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}),\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{2},w_{2}),t\right)\cdots\Psi(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{k-1},w_{k-1}))K_{Q_{k}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{k-1},w_{k-1}),\gamma x,t\right)\;dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1},

where
dydw
is the pullback volume form on the chart via the map
\phi_{\rm tub}
. Assume that
Q_{i}
is a Volterra operator of order
s_{i}
. Then in the tubular coordinates chart we have fixed at
x
this operator
Q_{i}
will correspond to an operator
in the euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{n}
that we call
Q^{\rm tub}_{i}
with symbol
q
; we can then write, with a small
but common abuse of notation,

(4.1)

K_{Q_{i}}(\phi_{\rm tub}(y,w),\phi_{\rm tub}(y^{\prime},w^{\prime}),t)\sim\sum_{j\geq 0}\check{q}_{s_{i}-j}(y,w,y^{\prime}-y,w^{\prime}-w,t).

Consider
x\in M^{\gamma}_{a}
and write accordingly
x=\phi_{\rm tub}(y,0)
.
We then consider

(4.2)

J(x,\gamma,t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-a}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{\times(k-1)}}K_{Q_{1}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y,w),\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}),t\right)\Psi(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}))\\
K_{Q_{2}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}),\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{2},w_{2}),t\right)\cdots\Psi(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{k-1},w_{k-1}))K_{Q_{k}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{k-1},w_{k-1}),\phi_{\rm tub}(y,d\gamma|_{y}(w)),t\right)\\
dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw,

where in the above we have used the property that
\gamma(\phi_{\rm tub}(y,w))=\phi_{\rm tub}(y,d\gamma_{y}(w))
.

For notational convenience, we will in the following developments combine the function
\Psi(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{i-1},w_{i-1}))
with
K_{Q_{i}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{i-1},w_{i-1}),\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{i},w_{i})),t\right)
by replacing
Q_{i}
with
\Psi(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{i-1},w_{i-1}))Q_{i}
so that the kernel of
Q_{i}
has compact support in the first component. And the integral (
4.2
) in the definition of
J(x,\gamma,t)
is reduced to a product of
\epsilon
-balls with a proper choice of
\epsilon>0
, i.e.

(4.3)

J(x,\gamma,t):=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n-a}(\epsilon)}\int_{{\mathbb{B}^{n}(\epsilon)}^{\times(k-1)}}K_{Q_{1}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y,w),\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}),t\right)\\
K_{Q_{2}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{1},w_{1}),\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{2},w_{2}),t\right)\cdots K_{Q_{k}}\left(\phi_{\rm tub}(y_{k-1},w_{k-1}),\phi_{\rm tub}(y,d\gamma|_{y}(w)),t\right)\\
dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw,

Lemma 4.4
.

Consider Volterra operators
Q_{i}
with (Volterra) order
m^{\prime}_{i}
,
i=1,\cdots,k
with symbol in tubular coordinates around
x\in M_{a}^{\gamma}
,

q_{i}\sim\sum_{s_{i}}q_{i,s_{i}},\ s_{i}\leq m^{\prime}_{i}.

Assume that the Schwartz kernel of each
Q_{i}
has compact support in the first component. Recall that
for
x\in M^{\gamma}_{a}
we have
x=\phi_{\rm tub}(y,0)
.
As
t\downarrow 0
, we have the following asymptotic expansion:

(4.5)

J(x,\gamma,t)\equiv J(y,\gamma,t)\sim\sum_{s_{i}\leq m^{\prime}_{i}}\sum_{\alpha_{i,s_{i}},\beta_{i,s_{i}}}t^{\frac{1}{2}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{k}s_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\left|\alpha_{i,s_{i}}\right|+|\beta_{i,s_{i}}|)-a\right)-k}\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-a}}\int_{\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)^{\times(k-1)}}\frac{y^{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}}w^{\beta_{1,s_{1}}}}{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}!\beta_{1,s_{1}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{1,s_{1}}}\check{q}_{1,s_{1}}\left(0,0,y_{1}-y,w_{1}-w,1\right)\\
\cdot\frac{y_{1}^{\alpha_{2,s_{2}}}w_{1}^{\beta_{2,s_{2}}}}{\alpha_{2,s_{2}}!\beta_{2,s_{2}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{2,s_{2}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{2,s_{2}}}\check{q}_{2,s_{2}}\left(0,0,y_{2}-y_{1},w_{2}-w_{1},1\right)\cdots\\
\cdots\frac{y_{k-1}^{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}}w_{k-1}^{\beta_{k,s_{k}}}}{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}!\beta_{k,s_{k}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{k,s_{k}}}\check{q}_{k,s_{k}}\left(0,0,y-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},1\right)dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw.

Here
\partial_{y}^{\alpha}
(and
\partial_{w}^{\beta}
) denotes differentiation with respect to the
y
(and
w
) variables and in
\check{q}
for a multi-indices
\alpha
(and
\beta
).

Proof.

By (
4.1
) and (
4.3
), we have the following expression of
J(y,\gamma,t)
,

(4.6)

J(y,\gamma,t)\sim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n-a}(\epsilon)}\int_{{\mathbb{B}^{n}(\epsilon)}^{\times(k-1)}}\left(\sum_{j_{1}\geq 0}\check{q}_{1,m^{\prime}_{1}-j_{1}}(y,w,y_{1}-y,w_{1}-w,t)\right)\\
\left(\sum_{j_{2}\geq 0}\check{q}_{2,m^{\prime}_{2}-j_{2}}(y_{1},w_{1},y_{2}-y_{1},w_{2}-w_{1},t)\right)\ldots\\
\left(\sum_{j_{k}\geq 0}\check{q}_{k,m^{\prime}_{k}-j_{k}}(y_{k-1},v_{k-1},y-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},t)\right)\;dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw

We need to work with general
(y,w)
instead of
(0,w)
to show that our
t
-growth estimate on
J(y,\gamma,t)
is continuous (uniformly) with respect to
y
in a tubular neighborhood of
x_{0}
. We replace
w_{i}
by
\sqrt{t}w_{i}
,
w
by
\sqrt{t}w
,
y_{i}
by
\sqrt{t}y_{i}
, and
y
by
\sqrt{t}y
. Then

(4.7)

J(y,\gamma,t)\sim t^{\frac{kn-a}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n-a}(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{t}})}\int_{{\mathbb{B}^{n}((\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{t}})}^{\times(k-1)}}\left(\sum_{j_{1}\geq 0}\check{q}_{1,m^{\prime}_{1}-j_{1}}(\sqrt{t}y,\sqrt{t}w,\sqrt{t}y_{1}-\sqrt{t}y,\sqrt{t}w_{1}-\sqrt{t}w,t)\right)\\
\left(\sum_{j_{2}\geq 0}\check{q}_{2,m^{\prime}_{2}-j_{2}}(\sqrt{t}y_{1},\sqrt{t}w_{1},\sqrt{t}y_{2}-\sqrt{t}y_{1},\sqrt{t}w_{2}-\sqrt{t}w_{1},t)\right)\ldots\\
\left(\sum_{j_{k}\geq 0}\check{q}_{k,m^{\prime}_{k}-j_{k}}(\sqrt{t}y_{k-1},\sqrt{t}v_{k-1},\sqrt{t}y-\sqrt{t}y_{k-1},\sqrt{t}d\gamma_{y}(w)-\sqrt{t}w_{k-1},t)\right)\;dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw

By the homogeneity of the symbols
q_{i,m^{\prime}_{i}-j_{i}}
(
i=1,...,k
), we see

(4.8)

J(y,\gamma,t)\sim t^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(j_{i}-m^{\prime}_{i})-a}{2}-k}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n-a}(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{t}})}\int_{{\mathbb{B}^{n}((\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{t}})}^{\times(k-1)}}\left(\sum_{j_{1}\geq 0}\check{q}_{1,m^{\prime}_{1}-j_{1}}(\sqrt{t}y,\sqrt{t}w,y_{1}-y,w_{1}-w,1)\right)\\
\left(\sum_{j_{2}\geq 0}\check{q}_{2,m^{\prime}_{2}-j_{2}}(\sqrt{t}y_{1},\sqrt{t}w_{1},y_{2}-y_{1},w_{2}-w_{1},1)\right)\ldots\\
\left(\sum_{j_{k}\geq 0}\check{q}_{k,m^{\prime}_{k}-j_{k}}(\sqrt{t}y_{k-1},\sqrt{t}v_{k-1},y-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},1)\right)\;dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw.

By Taylor’s formula, we can expand
\check{q}_{i,m^{\prime}_{i}-j_{i}}
(
i=1,...,k
) as follows,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\check{q}_{1,s_{1}}(\sqrt{t}y,\sqrt{t}w,y_{1}-y,w_{1}-w,1)=\sum_{\alpha_{1,s_{1}},\beta_{1,s_{1}}}t^{\frac{\left|\alpha_{1,s_{1}}\right|+\left|\beta_{1,s_{1}}\right|}{2}}\cdot\frac{y^{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}}w^{\beta_{1,s_{1}}}}{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}!\beta_{1,s_{1}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{1,s_{1}}}\check{q}_{1,s_{1}}\left(0,0,y_{1}-y,w_{1}-w,1\right),

and for
i=2,\cdots,k-1

(4.10)

\displaystyle\check{q}_{i,s_{i}}(\sqrt{t}y_{i-1},\sqrt{t}w_{i-1},y_{i}-y_{i-1},w_{i}-w_{i-1},1)

(4.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{i,s_{i}},\beta_{i,s_{i}}}t^{\frac{\left|\alpha_{i,s_{i}}\right|+\left|\beta_{i,s_{i}}\right|}{2}}\cdot\frac{y_{i-1}^{\alpha_{i,s_{i}}}w_{i-1}^{\beta_{i,s_{i}}}}{\alpha_{i,s_{i}}!\beta_{i,s_{i}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{i,s_{i}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{i,s_{i}}}\check{q}_{i,s_{i}}\left(0,0,y_{i}-y_{i-1},w_{i}-w_{i-1},1\right),

and

(4.12)

\check{q}_{k,s_{k}}(\sqrt{t}y_{k-1},\sqrt{t}v_{k-1},-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},1)\\
=\sum_{\alpha_{k,s_{k}},\beta_{k,s_{k}}}t^{\frac{\left|\alpha_{k,s_{k}}\right|+\left|\beta_{k,s_{k}}\right|}{2}}\cdot\frac{y_{k-1}^{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}}w_{k-1}^{\beta_{k,s_{k}}}}{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}!\beta_{k,s_{k}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{k,s_{k}}}\check{q}_{k,s_{k}}\left(0,0,-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},1\right).

Combining (
4.7
) - (
4.12
), we obtain the following

J(x,\gamma,t)\sim\sum_{s_{i}\leq m^{\prime}_{i}}\sum_{\alpha_{i,s_{i}},\beta_{i,\beta_{i}}}t^{\frac{1}{2}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{k}s_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\left|\alpha_{i,s_{i}}\right|+|\beta_{i,s_{i}}|)-a\right)-k}\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-a}}\int_{\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)^{\times(k-1)}}\frac{y^{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}}w^{\beta_{1,s_{1}}}}{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}!\beta_{1,s_{1}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{1,s_{1}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{1,s_{1}}}\check{q}_{1,s_{1}}\left(0,0,y_{1},w_{1}-w,1\right)\cdot\\
\frac{y_{1}^{\alpha_{2,s_{2}}}w_{1}^{\beta_{2,s_{2}}}}{\alpha_{2,s_{2}}!\beta_{2,s_{2}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{2,s_{2}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{2,s_{2}}}\check{q}_{2,s_{2}}\left(0,0,y_{2}-y_{1},w_{2}-w_{1},1\right)\cdots\\
\cdots\frac{y_{k-1}^{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}}w_{k-1}^{\beta_{k,s_{k}}}}{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}!\beta_{k,s_{k}}!}\partial^{\alpha_{k,s_{k}}}_{y}\partial_{w}^{\beta_{k,s_{k}}}\check{q}_{k,s_{k}}\left(0,0,y-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},1\right)dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw

which is what we wanted to prove.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

We point out that it follows from the symbol growth property (
A.5
) and the homogeneity property of the anti-Fourier transform of the symbol that the function
y^{\alpha}w^{\beta}\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\partial_{w}^{\beta}\check{q}_{i,s_{i}}(0,0,y,w)
has rapid decay property away from the origin. So the above integral in Lemma
4.4
converges absolutely.

Lemma 4.14
.

If
Q_{i}
has Getzler order
m_{i}
along
M^{\gamma}
and Volterra order
m^{\prime}_{i}
, then

\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(j)}\sim O(t^{\frac{-\sum_{i}m_{i}+j-a-2k}{2}}).

Proof.

To compute the Getzler order of
Q_{i}
, we look at the symbol
6
6

6

Notice that our notation for symbols (
q
in tubular coordinates and
\tilde{q}
in normal coordinates)
is reversed with respect to
[
Ponge-Wang-2
]

\tilde{q}_{i}(z,\eta)
of
Q_{i}
in the normal coordinate chart
(U,\varphi_{U})
centered at
x\in M^{\gamma}
via the exponential map
\exp_{x}:\mathbb{R}^{n}=T_{x}M=\mathbb{R}^{a}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-a}\to M
. Let
\kappa=\varphi_{U}\circ\phi_{\rm tub}:\mathbb{R}^{n}=\mathbb{R}^{a}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-a}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
d\kappa_{(y,w)}:T_{(y,w)}\mathbb{R}^{n}\to T_{\kappa(y,w)}\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the induced map between tangent spaces. We use
\theta_{(y,w)}
to denote the corresponding map
{d\kappa^{*}}^{-1}_{(y,w)}:T^{*}_{(y,w)}\mathbb{R}^{n}\to T^{*}_{\kappa(y,w)}\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

The change of variable formula for symbols
[
hormander-3
, Theorem 18.1.17]
gives

(4.15)

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{i}}q_{i,s_{i}},

\displaystyle\ \ \ q_{i,s_{i}}(y,w,\xi,\nu,\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}l_{i}-|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|=s_{i},\\
2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i}|\end{array}}}a_{i,l_{i},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}(y,w)\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{\eta}^{\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau),

where
a_{i,l_{i},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}
is a smooth function such that
a_{i,l_{i},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}=1
when
\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}}=\gamma_{i,l_{i}}=0
and
Q_{i}
has order
m_{i}^{\prime}
.

We compute the inverse Fourier transform of
q_{i,s_{i}}
in the
\xi,\nu,\tau
direction,

(4.16)

\displaystyle\check{q}_{i,s_{i}}(y,w,y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}l_{i}-|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|=s_{i},\\
2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|\end{array}}}a_{i,l_{i},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}(y,w)

\displaystyle\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{\eta}^{\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau)\big)^{\vee}(y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sum_{{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}l_{i}-|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|=s_{i},\\
2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|\end{array}}}\partial_{y}^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{w}^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}b_{i,l_{i},\alpha^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}(0,0)\frac{y^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}}{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}!}\frac{w^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}}{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}!}\frac{y^{\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}}{\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}!}\frac{w^{\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}}{\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}!}

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{y}^{\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{w}^{\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{\eta}^{\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau)|_{(y,w)=(0,0)}\big)^{\vee}(y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}l_{i}-|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|=s_{i},\\
2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|\end{array}}}\tilde{b}_{i,l_{i},\alpha^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}(0,0)y^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}w^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{y}^{\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{w}^{\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{\eta}^{\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau)|_{(y,w)=(0,0)}\big)^{\vee}(y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t).

Consider the following expression in the above expansion,

\partial_{y}^{\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{w}^{\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{\eta}^{\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau)|_{(y,w)=(0,0)}.

Applying the chain rule to compute the partial differentiation with respect to
y
and
w
, we obtain the following formula,

(4.17)

\begin{split}&\partial_{y}^{\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{w}^{\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\partial_{\eta}^{\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau)|_{(y,w)=(0,0)}\\
=&\sum_{|\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|=|\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|}g_{\delta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\sigma^{i}_{i,l_{i}}}\xi^{\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}(\kappa(y,w),\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu),\tau)\big)|_{(y,w)=(0,0)},\end{split}

where
g_{\delta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\upsilon_{i,l_{i}}}
is a constant. The inequality
|\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|=|\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|
is from the observation that the
\xi^{\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}
on the right side of (
4.17
) comes from the chain rule and differentiating the Jacobian
\theta_{(y,w)}(\xi,\nu)
with respect to the variables
y
and
w
. Furthermore, we notice that
\kappa(0,0)=0
and
d\kappa|_{(0,0)}={\rm Id}
.

Substituting the simplified expression (
4.17
) into the expansion (
4.16
) of
\check{q}_{i,s_{i}}(y,w,y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t)
, we obtain

(4.18)

\begin{split}&\check{q}_{i,s_{i}}(y,w,y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t)\\
=&\sum_{{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}l_{i}-|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|=s_{i},\\
2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|\end{array}}}a_{i,l_{i},\alpha^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}},\delta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\sigma^{j}_{i,l_{i}}}y^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}w^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\\
&\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}((0,0),(\xi,\nu),\tau)\big)^{\vee}(y^{\prime},w^{\prime},t).\end{split}

Applying (
4.18
) to the expression of
J(x,\gamma,t)
in (
4.5
), we obtain

(4.19)

\begin{split}\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(j)}\sim&\sum_{{\tiny\begin{array}[]{cc}2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|\end{array}}}t^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-l_{i}+|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|)-(a+2k)}{2}}\\
&\hskip 113.81102ptI^{\underline{j}}_{\underline{l},\underline{\alpha^{1}},\underline{\alpha^{2}},\underline{\alpha^{3}},\underline{\alpha^{4}},\underline{\beta^{1}},\underline{\beta^{2}},\underline{\delta^{1}},\underline{\delta^{2}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{\sigma^{1}},\underline{\sigma^{2}}},\end{split}

where
\underline{j}=(j_{1},\cdots,j_{k})
,
\underline{l}=(l_{1},\cdots,l_{k}),\ \underline{\alpha^{j}}=(\alpha^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\cdots,\alpha^{j}_{k,l_{k}}),\ j=1,2,3,4,\ \underline{\beta^{j}}=(\beta^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\cdots,\beta^{j}_{k,l_{k}}),\ l=1,2,\ \underline{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1,l_{1}},\cdots,\gamma_{k,l_{k}}),\ \underline{\delta^{j}}=(\delta^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\cdots,\delta^{j}_{k,l_{k}}),\ j=1,2,\ \underline{\sigma^{j}}=(\sigma^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\cdots,\sigma^{j}_{k,l_{k}})
,
j=1,2
, and

I_{\underline{l},\underline{\alpha^{1}},\underline{\alpha^{2}},\underline{\alpha^{3}},\underline{\alpha^{4}},\underline{\beta^{1}},\underline{\beta^{2}},\underline{\delta^{1}},\underline{\delta^{2}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{\sigma^{1}},\underline{\sigma^{2}}}\\
:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-a}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\times(k-1)}}a_{1,l_{1},\alpha^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\beta^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\gamma_{1,l_{1}},\delta^{j}_{1,l_{1}},\sigma^{j}_{1,l_{1}}}y^{\alpha^{1}_{1,l_{1}}+\alpha^{3}_{1,l_{1}}}w^{\alpha^{2}_{1,l_{1}}+\alpha^{4}_{1,l_{1}}}\\
\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{1,l_{1}}+\sigma^{1}_{1,l_{1}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{1,l_{1}}+\sigma^{2}_{1,l_{1}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{1,l_{1}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{1,l_{1}}+\gamma_{1,l_{1}}}\tilde{q}_{1,l_{1}}((0,0),(\xi,\nu),\tau)^{(j_{1})}\big)^{\vee}(y_{1}-y,w_{1}-w,1).\\
\prod_{i=2}^{k-1}a_{i,l_{i},\alpha^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}},\delta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\sigma^{j}_{i,l_{i}}}y_{i-1}^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}w_{i-1}^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\\
\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}((0,0),(\xi,\nu),\tau)^{(j_{i})}\big)^{\vee}(y_{i}-y_{i-1},w_{i}-w_{i-1},1).\\
a_{k,l_{k},\alpha^{j}_{k,l_{k}},\beta^{j}_{k,l_{k}},\gamma_{k,l_{k}},\delta^{j}_{k,l_{k}},\sigma^{j}_{k,l_{k}}}y_{k-1}^{\alpha^{1}_{k,l_{k}}+\alpha^{3}_{k,l_{k}}}w_{k-1}^{\alpha^{2}_{k,l_{k}}+\alpha^{4}_{k,l_{k}}}\\
\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}((0,0),(\xi,\nu),\tau)^{(j_{k})}\big)^{\vee}(y-y_{k-1},d\gamma_{y}(w)-w_{k-1},1).\\
dy_{1}dw_{1}\cdots dy_{k-1}dw_{k-1}dw,

and
j=j_{1}+\cdots+j_{k}
.

We observe that the term

y_{i-1}^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}w_{i-1}^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\\
\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}((0,0),(\xi,\nu),\tau)\big)^{\vee}(y_{i}-y_{i-1},w_{i}-w_{i-1},1)

is Getzler homogeneous of order

-|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|+l_{i}+j_{i}.

For any
i,l_{i},\alpha^{j}_{i,\lambda_{i}},\beta^{j}_{i,\lambda_{i}},\gamma_{i,l_{i}},\delta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\sigma^{j}_{i,l_{i}}
with

-|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|+l_{i}+j_{i}>m_{i},

the sum of terms

\begin{split}&\sum_{-|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|+l_{i}+j_{i}>m_{i}}a_{i,l_{i},\alpha^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\beta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\gamma_{k,l_{k}},\delta^{j}_{i,l_{i}},\sigma^{j}_{i,l_{i}}}y_{i-1}^{\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}}w_{i-1}^{\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}}\\
&\cdot\big(\xi^{\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}\nu^{\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\sigma^{2}_{i,l_{i}}}\big(\partial^{\delta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}}_{z}\partial_{\eta}^{\delta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}+\gamma_{i,l_{i}}}\tilde{q}_{i,l_{i}}((0,0),(\xi,\nu),\tau)\big)^{\vee}(y_{i}-y_{i-1},w_{i}-w_{i-1},1)\end{split}

must therefore vanish since the Getzler order of
q_{i}
is
m_{i}
.

In (
4.19
), we look at the power of
t
, that is

t^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-l_{i}+|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|)-(a+2k)}{2}}.

By the above observation using the Getzler order, we can assume to only consider those terms with

-|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|+l_{i}+j_{i}\leq m_{i}.

Using the property that
2(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|
from (
4.15
), we have

|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|\leq-\frac{1}{2}(|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|)\leq 0.

Hence, we only need to consider terms with the following bound on the sum,

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-l_{i}+|\beta^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\beta^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|-|\gamma_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{1}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{2}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{3}_{i,l_{i}}|+|\alpha^{4}_{i,l_{i}}|)-(a+2k)}{2}\geq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-m_{i}+j_{i})-(a+2k)}{2}.

∎

Definition 4.20
.

Let
A
be a smoothing operator with kernel
A(x,y)
. The geometric trace of
A
is defined by

(4.21)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(A\right):=\int_{M}A(y,y)\;dy,

if the integral on the right side is absolutely convergent. If
A
acts on the sections of a vector bundle
E
, then

(4.22)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(A\right):=\int_{M}{\rm tr}_{E_{y}}A(y,y)\;dy,

Lemma 4.23
.

If along
M^{\gamma}
the Getzler order of
Q_{i}
satisfies
\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}<-2k
, then

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}\Psi Q_{1}\Psi Q_{2}\Psi\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Proof.

Recall that

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}\Psi Q_{1}\Psi Q_{2}\Psi\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\int_{M}\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}\widetilde{J}(x,\gamma,t)\right]^{(n)}\;dx=\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\int_{M^{\gamma}}\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(n)}\;dy

The first equality follows from the definition of the function
\widetilde{J}(x,\gamma,t)
. The second equality follows from
J(y,\gamma,t)
by the definition of
J(y,\gamma,t)
and the property of
\Psi
, a function supported in a neighborhood of
y\in M^{\gamma}
.
In order to explain the last equality we make use of the last formula in page 337 in
[
Ponge-Wang-2
]

where our
\gamma
corresponds to
\phi^{S}
in that formula and
\mathfrak{a}(t)

corresponds to our
J(y,\gamma,t)
. Using also Lemma 4.7 in
[
Ponge-Wang-2
]
, and in particular formula (4.13) there, we obtain that

\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(n)}=\sigma[\gamma^{-1}]^{(0,n-a)}\wedge\sigma[J(y,\gamma,t]^{(a,0)}

modulo terms involving
\sigma J(y,\gamma,t)^{(a,l)}
with
l\geq 1
.

Recall
[
Ponge-Wang-2
, Lemma 4.7]
that the operator
(\gamma^{-1})^{\mathcal{S}}\otimes(\gamma^{-1})^{E}
is a pointwise operator on the fiber
\mathcal{S}|_{y}\otimes E|_{y}
of the form

(\gamma^{-1})^{\mathcal{S}}=\coprod_{\frac{a}{2}<j\leq\frac{n}{2}}\left(\cos\left(\frac{\theta_{j}}{2}\right)+\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{j}}{2}\right)c(v^{2j-1})c(v^{2j})\right),

where
v_{1},\cdots,v_{a}
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{a}
, and
v_{a+1},\cdots,v_{n}
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{n-a}
, and
e^{i\theta_{a+1}},\cdots,e^{i\theta_{n}}
are the eigenvalues of the action of
\gamma^{-1}
on
\mathcal{S}|_{y}
. Hence, the expression
\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(n)}
has the following form

\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(n)}=\sigma[\gamma^{-1}]^{(0,n-a)}\wedge\sigma[J(y,\gamma,t]^{(a,0)}+\sum_{0<l\leq n-a}\sigma[\gamma^{-1}]^{(0,n-l)}\wedge\sigma[J(y,\gamma,t)]^{(a,l)}.

Recall now Lemma
4.14
stating that

\sigma\left[J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(j)}\sim O(t^{\frac{-\sum_{i}m_{i}+j-a-2k}{2}}).

This means that

\sigma\left[J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(a,l)}\sim O(t^{\frac{-\sum_{i}m_{i}+l-2k}{2}})\;\;\;\text{where}\;\;\;l\geq 0.

Hence, if the Getzler order
\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}<-2k
, then

\sigma\left[J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(a,l)}=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{t})\;\;\;\text{for all }\;\;\;l\geq 0.

This shows that
\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}J(y,\gamma,t)\right]^{(n)}\to 0
as
t\to 0
, which completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following proposition will play a key role in moving terms under the sign
of
\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}
. First we give two Definitions.

Definition 4.24
.

We shall say that a family of smoothing kernels
\{Q(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
on
M\times M
defined by a smoothing kernel
K_{Q}(x,y,t)
on
M\times M\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
is
Volterra-related
if there exists a Volterra operator with Volterra kernel
equal to
K_{Q}
when restricted to
M\times M\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
. The Volterra order (Getzler order at
x_{0}
) of
\{Q(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}

is by definition the Volterra order (Getzler order at
x_{0}
) of the corresponding Volterra operator.

For example, the heat-kernel is Volterra-related; its Volterra order is -2 and its Getzler order
at any point of
M
is also -2. More examples will be given in the next subsection.

Definition 4.25
.

We shall say that a family of smoothing kernels
\{Q(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
on
M\times M
defined by a smoothing kernel
K_{Q}(x,y,t)
on
M\times M\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
is of
exponential control
if there exist constants
\alpha,\beta,\eta>0
such that for sufficiently small
t
,

(4.26)

\displaystyle\Big|K_{Q}(x,y,t)\Big|\leq\alpha\cdot t^{-\beta}\cdot e^{-\eta\cdot\frac{d(x,y)}{t}}.

In what follows, we sometimes will abuse notation and employ the simplified symbol
Q

instead of
\{Q(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
.

Lemma 4.27
.

If
\{Q_{1}(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
and
\{Q_{2}(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
have smoothing kernels
K_{Q_{i}}(x,y,t)
that are of exponential control, the composition of
\{Q_{1}(t)\circ Q_{2}(t)\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
is well defined and has a smoothing kernel of exponential control.

Proof.

Since the smoothing kernels
K_{Q_{i}}(x,y,t)
(
i=1,2
) are of exponential control, there are
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},\eta_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
,

\Big|K_{Q_{i}}(x,y,t)\Big|\leq\alpha_{i}\cdot t^{-\beta_{i}}\cdot e^{-\eta_{i}\cdot\frac{d(x,y)}{t}}.

Take
\eta=\min(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
. By Lemma
3.14
, for sufficiently small
t
,

\int_{M}e^{-\eta_{1}\frac{d(x,z)}{2t}}e^{-\eta_{2}\frac{d(z,y)}{2t}}dz\leq\int_{M}e^{-\eta\frac{d(x,z)}{2t}}e^{-\eta\frac{d(z,y)}{2t}}dz

is integrable. We can derive the exponential control property of the composition by the following observation

|\int_{M}K_{Q_{1}}(x,z,t)K_{Q_{2}}(z,y,t)dy|\leq\int_{M}|K_{Q_{1}}(x,z,t)K_{Q_{2}}(z,y,t)|dy\leq Ct^{-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}}e^{-\eta\frac{d(x,y)}{2t}}\int_{M}e^{-\eta\frac{d(x,z)}{2t}}e^{-\eta\frac{d(z,y)}{2t}}dz.

∎

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
f_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
. We assume that we have
Volterra-related smoothing kernels
Q_{i}
satisfying the exponential control property
and such that

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}\Psi Q_{2}\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0,

for a compactly supported smooth function
\Psi
, which is equal to
1
on the support of
f_{0}
. Then

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}Q_{2}\ldots Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Proof.

Using partition of unit, we can write

f_{0}(y)=\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(y)f_{0}(y).

We can choose the support of
\rho_{i}
to be arbitrarily small. Thus, we can assume that
f_{0}
is supported in a small neighborhood
U_{y}
for some
y\in M
. Shrinking the support of
f_{0}
if necessary, we can find a small
\epsilon>0
and a function
\Psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
such that

(4.29)

\supp(\Psi)\subseteq U_{y},\quad\Psi\equiv 1\text{ on }B\left(\supp(f_{0}),\epsilon\right).

By assumption, we have

(4.30)

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}\Psi Q_{2}\Psi\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Moreover, we know that ,

\left\|K_{Q_{i}}(x,y,t)\right\|\leq\alpha_{i}\cdot t^{-\beta_{i}}\cdot e^{-\eta_{i}\cdot\frac{d(x,y)}{t}}

for some positive constants
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},\eta_{i}
. By the condition in (
4.29
),

f_{0}(x)(1-\Psi)(y)=0

whenever
\operatorname{dist}(x,y)<\epsilon
. Thus

\left\|f_{0}(x)\cdot K_{Q_{1}}(x,y,t)\cdot(1-\Psi)(y)\right\|\leq\alpha_{1}\cdot t^{-\beta_{1}}\cdot e^{-\eta_{1}\cdot\frac{\epsilon+d(x,y)}{2t}}

It follows that

(4.31)

\left|\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}(1-\Psi)Q_{2}\Psi\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)\right|\\
\leq\int_{U_{y}\times M\times U_{y}^{\times(k-2)}}\left\|f_{0}(x_{0})\cdot K_{Q_{1}}(x_{0},y,t)\cdot(1-\Psi)(y)K_{Q_{2}}(y,x_{2},t)\Psi(x_{2})\ldots\Psi(x_{l})K_{Q_{l}}(x_{l},\gamma x_{0},t)\right\|dx_{0}dydx_{2}\ldots dx_{k}\\
\leq C\int_{U_{y}\times M}\left\|f_{0}(x_{0})\cdot K_{Q_{1}}(x_{0},y,t)(1-\Psi)(y)\right\|dx_{0}dy\\
\leq C\alpha t^{-\beta}\cdot e^{-\eta\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{2t}}\int_{U_{y}\times M}e^{-\eta\cdot\frac{d(x_{0},y)}{t}}dx_{0}dy.

By Lemma
3.14
, we know that

\int_{U_{y}\times M}e^{-\eta\cdot\frac{d(x_{0},y)}{t}}dx_{0}dy

is uniformly bounded in the
t
-variable, for
t\leq 1
.
Because of the presence of the factor
e^{-\eta\frac{\epsilon}{2t}}
in (
4.31
), we have shown

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}(1-\Psi)Q_{2}\Psi\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Together with (
4.30
) we obtain

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}Q_{2}\Psi\ldots\Psi Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0,

Note now that it follows from Lemma
4.27
that the composition
Q_{1}Q_{2}
satisfies the exponential-control condition as well, thus we can repeat the argument and conclude by induction that

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}Q_{2}\ldots Q_{l}^{\gamma}\right)=0.

∎

Theorem 4.32
.

Suppose that
Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{k}
are Volterra-related smoothing kernels
on
M\times M\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
that are of exponential control and that are of order
m^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,m^{\prime}_{k}
and Getzler order along the fixed point set
M^{\gamma}
equal to
m_{1},\cdots,m_{k}
. If
m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k}<-2k
, then for any
f_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
,

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}Q_{1}Q_{2}\ldots Q_{k}^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Proof.

This result follows from Lemmas
4.4
,
4.14
,
4.23
,
4.28
.
∎

4.2.
Some useful Volterra operators

In this subsection we prove that the kernels appearing in the index pairing via the Connes-Moscovici
projector for
tD
are indeed Volterra-related and we compute their Getzler order.
Our results are summarized in the table at the end of the subsection.

Let us consider the Volterra operator

(4.33)

Q=\left(\frac{1}{2}D^{2}+\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\frac{3}{2}D^{2}+\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\right)^{-1}.

Notice that this operator has Getzler order -4.
We denote by
K_{Q}(x,y,t)
the distributional kernel of
Q
. We also consider
the operator

t\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}

for
t>0
and we denote by
\kappa_{t}(x,y)
its kernel.

Lemma 4.34
.

The identity

(4.35)

t\,\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}t\,e^{-s_{1}tD^{2}}\;ds_{1},

holds true and its kernel
\kappa_{t}(x,y)
is of rapid exponential decay.

Proof.

Let

h_{t}(x):=\left(\frac{1-e^{-tx}}{tx}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}x}.

We then have that the operator on the left hand side of (
4.35
) is equal to
h_{t}(D^{2})
, the bounded operator obtained by applying the Borel functional calculus to the selfadjoint operator
D^{2}
.
That is,

\left<\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\right>=\int_{0}^{\infty}h_{t}(\lambda)dE_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\lambda),\qquad\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in L^{2}(M),

where
E
denotes the resolution of the identity on
\mathbb{R}
determined by
D^{2}
. Because of the identity

(4.36)

\left(\frac{1-e^{-tx}}{tx}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}x}=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-stx}ds,

we therefore have

\left<\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\right>=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-st\lambda}dsdE_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\lambda).

Write
\mu=ds\times dE_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
for the product measure on
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
and notice that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]}e^{-st\lambda}d|\mu(x,y)|\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]}e^{-\frac{t\lambda}{2}}d|\mu(x,y)|=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t\lambda}{2}}d|E_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}|=\left|\left<e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\right>\right|<\infty.

We can therefore apply Fubini’s theorem and change the order of integration:

\left<\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\right>=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-st\lambda}dE_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\lambda)ds=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\left<e^{-stD^{2}}\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\right>ds.

Denoting by
K_{t}(x,y)
the usual heat kernel, we see from this equality that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\psi(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}(e^{-stD^{2}}\psi)(x)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{M}K_{st}(x,y)\psi(y)dyds

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}K_{st}(x,y)\psi(y)dsdy,

where we used Fubini again, this time using the fact that
K_{t}(x,y)
is of exponential rapid decay in
y
and
[1/2,3/2]
is compact.
This shows that the kernel of the operator in (
4.35
) is given by

\kappa_{t}(x,y)=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}K_{st}(x,y)ds.

From this we deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{x,y}\left|e^{qd(x,y)}K^{\prime}_{t}(x,y)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{x,y}\left|e^{qd(x,y)}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}K_{st}(x,y)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x,y}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|e^{qd(x,y)}K_{st}(x,y)\right|ds<\infty.

This proves that the kernel
\kappa_{t}(x,y)
is of rapid exponential decay.
∎

Let us go back to the Volterra operator
Q
in (
4.33
) and let
K_{Q}
be its Volterra
kernel.

Lemma 4.37
.

We have that for
t>0

t\cdot\kappa_{t}(x,y)=K_{Q}(x,y,t).

Proof.

For any
u\in C^{\infty}_{+}(\mathbb{R},L^{2}(M,S))
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}D^{2}+\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\frac{3}{2}D^{2}+\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\right)^{-1}u(x,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t_{0}\frac{1}{2}D^{2}}\cdot e^{-t_{1}\frac{3}{2}D^{2}}u(t-t_{0}-t_{1})dt_{0}dt_{1}

\displaystyle\text{let }\sigma=t_{0}+t_{1},s_{0}=\frac{t_{0}}{\sigma},s_{1}=\frac{t_{1}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\sigma\cdot e^{-(1-s_{1})\frac{\sigma}{2}D^{2}}\cdot e^{-s_{1}\frac{3\sigma}{2}D^{2}}u(t-\sigma)\;ds_{1}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\sigma\cdot e^{-s_{1}\sigma D^{2}}u(t-\sigma)\;ds_{1}d\sigma

The lemma follows from Lemma
4.34
.
∎

Lemma 4.38
.

At any
x_{0}\in M
, the Getzler order of
[D^{2},f]
is 1 and that of
[D^{2},c(df)]
is 2.

Proof.

In terms of synchronous normal coordinates, we write

D=\sum c(e_{i})\nabla_{e_{i}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle[D^{2},f]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Dc(df)+c(df)D=\sum c(e_{i})\nabla_{e_{i}}c(df)+c(df)\sum c(e_{i})\nabla_{e_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum c(e_{i})c(\nabla_{e_{i}}df)+\sum\left(c(df)c(e_{i})+c(e_{i})c(df)\right)\nabla_{e_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum c(e_{i})c(e_{j})e_{i}e_{j}(f)+\sum e_{j}(f)\left(c(e_{j})c(e_{i})+c(c_{i})c(e_{j})\right)\nabla_{e_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\operatorname{Hess}(f)-2\sum\left(\partial_{i}f\right)\cdot\nabla_{e_{i}}.

Here
\operatorname{Hess}(f)=\sum_{i}e_{i}e_{i}(f)
is a scalar multiplication with Getzler order 0 and
\left(\partial_{i}f\right)\cdot\nabla_{e_{i}}
has order one.

For the term
[D^{2},c(df)]
, we recall the Weitzenböck identity,

D^{2}=-\sum\nabla_{e_{i}}\nabla_{e_{i}}+\frac{1}{4}\kappa+F^{S}.

The later two terms commutes with
c(df)
. Thus,

[D^{2},c(df)]=-\sum[\nabla_{e_{i}}\nabla_{e_{i}},c(df)]=-\sum\nabla_{e_{i}}\nabla_{e_{i}}c(df)+\sum c(df)\nabla_{e_{i}}\nabla_{e_{i}}\\
=-\sum\nabla_{e_{i}}c(df)\nabla_{e_{i}}-\sum\nabla_{e_{i}}c(\nabla_{e_{i}}df)+\sum c(df)\nabla_{e_{i}}\nabla_{e_{i}}\\
=-\sum c(\nabla_{e_{i}}df)\nabla_{e_{i}}-\sum\nabla_{e_{i}}c(\nabla_{e_{i}}df),

which shows it has order two.
∎

5.
The higher Lefschetz formula

5.1.
Index pairing

Now we consider

\Ind_{{\rm exp}}(D):=[V(tD)]-[e_{1}\oplus e_{1}]\in K_{0}(\mathcal{A}^{{\rm exp}}_{\Gamma}(M,E)).

where
e_{1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)
and

V(tD)=V_{{\rm CM}}(tD)\oplus(V_{{\rm CM}}(tD))^{*}

is the symmetrized Connes-Moscovici cocycle. Take
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\gamma}^{\bullet}(\Gamma)
of (at most) EG. By Prop.
3.15
,
\rho\circ\Psi(c)
extends continuously from
\mathcal{A}^{c}_{\Gamma}(M)
to
\mathcal{A}^{{\rm exp}}_{\Gamma}(M)
; consequently, by Diagram
2.10
, also
\Phi(\tau_{c})
extends and

(5.1)

\langle[V(tD)]-[e_{1}\oplus e_{1}],\Phi(\tau_{c})\rangle=\langle[V(tD)]-[e_{1}\oplus e_{1}],\rho\circ\Psi(c)\rangle.

We are interested in proving a formula for the pairing of the class
\Ind_{{\rm exp}}(D)
with the cyclic cocycle
\rho(\Psi(c))\in HC^{2p}(\mathcal{A}^{{\rm exp}}_{G}(M))
, that is the right hand side of the above equation.

Since the
K
-theory class
[V(tD)]-[e_{1}\oplus e_{1}]
is independent of
t
, we can compute the above pairing by taking the limit
t\to 0
.

\lim_{t\to 0}\langle[V(tD)]-[e_{1}\oplus e_{1}],\rho\circ\Psi(c)\rangle

Our computation mainly adapts the method in
[
CM
]
to the equivariant setting and to Volterra operators.

Recall from Moscovici-Wu that the class defined by
V_{{\rm CM}}(tD)\oplus(V_{{\rm CM}}(tD))^{*}
and
e_{1}\oplus e_{1}
,
t>0
, is equal to the class
defined by the
4\times 4
matrix

R(tD):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}e^{-tD^{2}}\epsilon&\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\sqrt{t}D\epsilon\\
e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}\sqrt{t}D\epsilon&e^{-tD^{2}}\epsilon\end{array}\right)

with
\epsilon
the grading operator on the Clifford bundle
E
.

By the computation in (
3.2
),

(5.2)

\rho\circ\Psi(c)(R(\sqrt{t}D),\ldots,R(\sqrt{t}D))\\
=\sum_{I=(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{2p})\in\Gamma^{\times(2p+1)}}c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{2p})\cdot\chi_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{1}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{2p}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)

where
A^{\gamma}(x,y):=A(x,\gamma y),f_{i}(y_{i})=\chi(\gamma_{i}^{-1}y_{i})
. By the discussion in Section
3.4
, Lemma
3.59
, we consider the anti-symmetrized expression

(5.3)

\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}_{a}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{1}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{2p}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)\\
:=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2p+1}}\text{sign}(\sigma)\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(0)}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{\sigma(1)}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{\sigma(2p)}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right).

In particular, we have that

(5.4)

\rho\circ\Psi(c)(R(\sqrt{t}D),\ldots,R(\sqrt{t}D))\\
=\frac{1}{(2p+1)!}\sum_{I=(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{2p})\in\Gamma^{\times(2p+1)}}c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{2p})\cdot\chi_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}_{a}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{1}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{2p}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=\frac{1}{(2p+1)!}\sum_{\text{finitely many }\mathcal{I}}c(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{2p})\cdot\chi_{\gamma}(\gamma_{0})\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}_{a}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{1}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{2p}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)+O(t^{\infty}).

Lemma 5.5
.

The following identity holds:

(5.6)

\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}_{a}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{1}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{2p}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2p+1}}\text{sign}(\sigma)\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(0)}\left[R(\sqrt{t}D),f_{\sigma(1)}\right]\ldots\left[R(\sqrt{t}D),f_{\sigma(2p)}\right]R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)

Proof.

Since

(5.7)

\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(0)}f_{\sigma(1)}R(\sqrt{t}D)R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{\sigma(2)}\cdots f_{\sigma(2p)}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(1)}f_{\sigma(0)}R(\sqrt{t}D)R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{\sigma(2)}\cdots f_{\sigma(2p)}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right),

it follows from the anti-symmetrization that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2p+1}}\text{sign}(\sigma)\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(0)}f_{\sigma(1)}R(\sqrt{t}D)R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{\sigma(2)}\cdots f_{\sigma(2p)}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Thus,

(5.9)

\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}_{a}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{1}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{2p}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2p+1}}\text{sign}(\sigma)\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(0)}R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{\sigma(1)}R(\sqrt{t}D)\cdots f_{\sigma(2p)}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2p+1}}\text{sign}(\sigma)\cdot\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{\sigma(0)}\left[R(\sqrt{t}D),f_{\sigma(1)}\right]R(\sqrt{t}D)\ldots R(\sqrt{t}D)f_{\sigma(2p)}R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma}\right).

By repeating the procedure, we can prove the lemma.
∎

Our goal is to show that we can take
the limit of this expression at
t\downarrow 0
and that this limit is given by a higher Atiyah-Segal-Singer formula.
Following
[
moscovici-wu
]
we introduce the following
Volterra-related operators:

A^{+}:=e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}D\,\text{ with Getzler order 0},\quad A^{-}:=t\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}D\text{ with Getzler order -2}

B^{+}_{i}:=e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}[D,f_{i}]\,\text{ with Getzler order -1},\quad B^{-}_{i}:=t\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}[D,f_{i}]\text{ with Getzler order -3}

C^{+}_{i}=[e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}},f_{i}]D\,,\text{ with Getzler order -1}\quad C^{-}_{i}:=\left[t\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}},f_{i}\right]D\text{ with Getzler order -3}

and then

A=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\frac{A^{-}}{\sqrt{t}}\\
\sqrt{t}A^{+}&0\end{array}\right)\,,\quad B_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\frac{B^{-}_{i}}{\sqrt{t}}\\
\sqrt{t}B^{+}_{i}&0\end{array}\right)\,,\quad C_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\frac{C^{-}_{i}}{\sqrt{t}}\\
\sqrt{t}C^{+}_{i}&0\end{array}\right).

We notice once again that
A^{\pm}
,
B_{i}^{\pm}
, and
C_{i}^{\pm}
are all
Volterra-related operators. In what follows we will use
\operatorname{ord}_{G}
to denote the Getzler order of a Volterra operator. We point out that the Getzler order is, in general, a number depending on
x_{0}\in M^{\gamma}
but for
A^{\pm}
,
B_{i}^{\pm}
, and
C_{i}^{\pm}
the Getzler order is actually constant, independent of
x_{0}
in
M^{\gamma}
.

We then have

\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}[R(\sqrt{t}D),f_{1}]\cdots[R(\sqrt{t}D),f_{2p}]R(\sqrt{t}D)^{\gamma})=\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}(\Pi(\sqrt{t}D))

with

\Pi(\sqrt{t}D):=\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\left(\Pi_{i=1}^{2p}\left(\left[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{i}\right]\cdot I_{2}\epsilon+B_{i}\epsilon+C_{i}\epsilon\right)\right)(e^{-tD^{2}}\cdot I_{2}\epsilon+A\epsilon)^{\gamma},

and

I_{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)
.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{\rm geo}
denote the supertrace on the space of smoothing kernels with finite propagation.
The terms that contribute to

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}\!\big(\Pi(\sqrt{t}D)\big)

are precisely the following:

(1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}_{s}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}\epsilon B_{2}\epsilon\cdots B_{2q-1}\epsilon B_{2q}\epsilon\,(e^{-tD^{2}})^{\gamma}\right)=(-1)^{q}\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}_{s}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}B_{2}\cdots B_{2q-1}B_{2q}\,(e^{-tD^{2}})^{\gamma}\right);

(2)

\begin{split}&\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}_{s}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}\epsilon B_{2}\epsilon\cdots C_{j}\epsilon\cdots B_{2q-1}\epsilon B_{2q}\epsilon\,(e^{-tD^{2}})^{\gamma}\right)\\
=&(-1)^{q}\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}_{s}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}B_{2}\cdots C_{j}\cdots B_{2q-1}B_{2q}\,(e^{-tD^{2}})^{\gamma}\right);\end{split}

(3)

\begin{split}&\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}_{s}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}\epsilon B_{2}\epsilon\cdots[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}]I_{2}\epsilon\cdots B_{2q-1}\epsilon B_{2q}\epsilon\,A^{\gamma}\right)\\
=&(-1)^{q+j+1}\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}_{s}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}B_{2}\cdots[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}]I_{2}\cdots B_{2q-1}B_{2q}\,A^{\gamma}\right).\end{split}

Proof.

In the expansion of
\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm{geo}}\!\big(\Pi(\sqrt{t}D)\big)
,
the
off-diagonal terms
are given by
B_{i}^{\pm}
,
C_{i}^{\pm}
, and
A^{\pm}
,
while the
diagonal terms
are

[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{i}]\quad\text{of Getzler order }-3,\qquad e^{-tD^{2}}\quad\text{of Getzler order }-2.

As there are equal number of
+
and
-
terms in the product expansion, we observe that the factor of
t
in
A
,
B
,
C
cancel. We will use Theorem
4.32
to show that there are only a few types of expression that could survive in the small time limit
t\to 0
.

We distinguish two possibilities.

(I) Terms ending with a diagonal factor
e^{-tD^{2}}
.

We observe that such a term must contain an
even
,
2p
, number of diagonal commutators
[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{i}]
, whose Getzler order of each commutator is -3, and in terms from
B
and
C
the number of
+
terms appearing must equal the number of
-
terms appearing. Notice that the
B_{i}^{+}
and
C_{i}^{+}
are
Volterra-related operators with Getzler order -1 and
B_{i}^{-}
and
C_{i}^{-}
are Volterra-related operators with Getzler order -3. The total sum of Getzler order of such a term is

(-3)\times 2p+(-2)\times(2q-2p)-2=-2p-4q-2.

To maximize the total order, we therefore consider terms with
p=0
.

Hence the only possible terms are of the form

\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,(B_{1}\text{ or }C_{1})\cdots(B_{2q}\text{ or }C_{2q})\,(e^{-tD^{2}})^{\gamma}.

Note that each
C_{j}
contains one factor of
D
.
If more than one
C_{j}
appears, there will be at least two
D
’s.
By Theorem
4.32
, we may move these
D
’s together without increasing the Getzler order.
Since

\operatorname{ord}_{G}(D^{2})=2<\operatorname{ord}_{G}(D)+\operatorname{ord}_{G}(D)=4,

such terms are strictly of lower order.
Hence, at most one
C_{j}
may appear.
The total order of the contributing terms is therefore

q\times(-4)+(-2)=-4q-2.

(II) Terms ending with an off-diagonal factor
A^{\pm}
.

Here, the expression must contain an
odd
number,
2p+1
, of diagonal commutators
[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{i}]
.
By the same reasoning as Case (I), the maximal order occurs when exactly one such commutator appears:

\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,(B_{1}\text{ or }C_{1})\cdots\big([e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}]\big)\cdots(B_{2q}\text{ or }C_{2q})\,A^{\gamma}.

Since
A
already contains a factor of
D
, moving
D
using Theorem
4.32
shows that the presence of any
C_{j}^{\pm}
between
\big([e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}]\big)
and
A
would further lower the Getzler order. Here if
C_{j}
appears before
\big([e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}]\big)
, we use the fact that

\left[D,\left[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}\right]\right]=\left[e^{-tD^{2}},\left[D,f_{j}\right]\right]=\left[e^{-tD^{2}},c(df_{j})\right]

and

-2=\operatorname{ord}_{G}\left(\left[e^{-tD^{2}},c(df_{j})\right]\right)<\operatorname{ord}_{G}\left(D\left[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}\right]\right)=-1.

Therefore, only the case with no
C_{j}
’s contributes:

\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\,B_{1}B_{2}\cdots\big([e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}]\big)\cdots B_{2q-1}B_{2q}\,A^{\gamma}.

Its total order is

-3+(q-1)\times(-4)+1+(-4)=-3+(q-1)\times(-4)+0+(-3)=-4q-2.

Hence, the only terms that contribute to the limit are exactly those listed above.
∎

Definition 5.11
.

We define the following four types of integrals:

(1)

Type I:

T_{I}(\sqrt{t}D)\colon=\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\mp}_{1}B^{\pm}_{2}\cdots B_{2q-1}^{\mp}B_{2q}^{\pm}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma};

(2)

Type II:

T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\colon=\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\pm}_{1}B^{\mp}_{2}\cdots C_{j}^{-}\cdots B^{\pm}_{2q-1}B^{\mp}_{2q}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma};

(3)

Type III:

T_{III,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\colon=\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\pm}_{1}B^{\mp}_{2}\cdots C_{j}^{+}\cdots B^{\pm}_{2q-1}B^{\mp}_{2q}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma};

(4)

Type IV:

T_{IV,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\colon=\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\mp}_{1}B^{\pm}_{2}\cdots\left[e^{-tD^{2}},f_{j}\right]\cdots B^{-}_{2q-1}B^{+}_{2q}\left(A^{\pm}\right)^{\gamma}.

We want to compute the limit of the trace of each term as
t\downarrow 0
.

5.2.
Computation of type I

Put

B^{+}=e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}},\quad B^{-}=t\frac{1-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}.

We have that

B_{i}^{-}=B^{-}\sigma(df_{i}),\ \ \ B_{i}^{+}=B^{+}\sigma(df_{i}).

Lemma 5.12
.

We have that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(T_{I}(\sqrt{t}D)\right)=\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\ldots c(df_{2q})\cdot B^{\pm}B^{\mp}\ldots B^{\mp}\cdot\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right),

Proof.

We write

(5.14)

\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B_{1}^{\pm}B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)=\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\pm}c(df_{1})B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})B^{\pm}B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)+\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\left[B^{\pm},c(df_{1})\right]B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right).

Since

\operatorname{ord}_{G}\left(\left[B^{\pm},c(df_{1})\right]\right)\leq\operatorname{ord}_{G}\left(B^{\pm}\right),

the second term in the above equation satisfies the assumptions in Theorem
4.32
,

\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}\left[B^{\pm},c(df_{1})\right]B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)=0.

Hence,

\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\pm}_{1}B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)=\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})B^{\pm}B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)

We prove the lemma by repeating the argument.

\begin{split}&\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{\pm}_{1}B_{2}^{\mp}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)=\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})c(df_{2})B^{\pm}B^{\mp}B^{\pm}_{3}\ldots B_{2q}^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)\\
=&\cdots=\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})c(df_{2})\cdots c(df_{2q})B^{\pm}B^{\mp}B^{\pm}\ldots B^{\mp}\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right)\end{split}

∎

Lemma 5.15
.

We have that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(T_{I}(\sqrt{t}D)\right)=\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})B^{\gamma}\right),

where

(5.17)

\displaystyle B=\int_{1}^{2}\ldots\int_{1}^{2}e^{-t\left(1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}ds_{1}\cdots ds_{q}.

Proof.

Recall (
4.35
) that

t\,\left(\frac{I-e^{-tD^{2}}}{tD^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{t}{2}D^{2}}=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}t\,e^{-s_{1}tD^{2}}\;ds_{1}.

Since
B^{-}
commutes with
B^{+}
, we can rewrite

B^{\pm}B^{\mp}\ldots B^{\mp}\cdot\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\\
=t^{q}\left(\int_{1/2}^{3/2}\ldots\int_{1/2}^{3/2}e^{-t\left(\frac{q}{2}+1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}ds_{1}\cdots ds_{q}\right)^{\gamma}\\
=t^{q}\left(\int_{1}^{2}\ldots\int_{1}^{2}e^{-t\left(1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}ds_{1}\cdots ds_{q}\right)^{\gamma}.

∎

Lemma 5.18
.

\begin{split}&\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})B^{\gamma}\right)\\
=&\int_{1}^{2}\ldots\int_{1}^{2}\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})e^{-t\left(1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}ds_{1}\cdots ds_{q}.\end{split}

Proof.

Given
(s_{1},\cdots,s_{q})\in[1,2]^{\times q}
, consider the operator
\widetilde{Q}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{q})
by

\widetilde{Q}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{q})=P\Big((1+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{q})D^{2}+\partial_{t}\Big)^{-1},

where
P=f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})
. Since
f_{0},\cdots,f_{q}
are compactly supported, the operator
\widetilde{Q}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{q})
is a trace class operator. Let
\mathcal{L}^{1}
be the Banach space of trace class operators. We observe that the
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-valued function
\widetilde{Q}:[1,2]^{\times q}\to\mathcal{L}^{1}
is continuous. Hence,

\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})B^{\gamma}\right)=\int_{1}^{2}\ldots\int_{1}^{2}t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})e^{-t\left(1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}ds_{1}\cdots ds_{q}.

Notice that the
t
-asymptotic estimate using the Getzler order is uniform on any compact set, c.f. Theorem
A.15
,
A.22
,
A.23
, Lemma
4.4
,
4.14
. Consider
Q=P(D^{2}+\partial_{t})^{-1}
. Then the smoothing (Schwartz) kernels of
Q(s)
and
Q
satisfy the following relation,

Q(s)(x,y,t)=s^{-1}Q(x,y,st),\quad s,t>0.

Accordingly, the
t
-asymptotic estimate of
\widetilde{Q}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{q})(x,\gamma x,t)
is uniform on
(s_{1},\cdots,s_{q})\in[1,2]^{\times q}
and
x
. This allows us to commute the limit of
t\downarrow 0
with the integral over
[0,1]^{\times q}
and get

\begin{split}&\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})B^{\gamma}\right)\\
=&\int_{1}^{2}\ldots\int_{1}^{2}\lim_{t\downarrow 0^{+}}t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})e^{-t\left(1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}ds_{1}\cdots ds_{q}.\end{split}

∎

We now evaluate the limit
t\downarrow 0
of the term

t^{q}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})e^{-t\left(1+s_{1}+\ldots+s_{q}\right)D^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}.

Set
s=1+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{q}
. Consider

Q(s)=P(sD^{2}+\partial_{t})^{-1},

where
P=f_{0}c(df_{1})\cdots c(df_{2q})
is a differential operator with Getzler order
2q
and

P_{(2q)}=f_{0}df_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge f_{2q}.

Write

K_{Q(s)}(x,y,t)

the smoothing kernel of
Q(s)
.

For any
x\in M^{\gamma}_{a}
, we have defined

I_{Q(s)}(x,t,\gamma):=\int_{N_{x}^{\gamma}(\epsilon)}K_{Q(s)}\left(\exp_{x}v,\exp_{x}\left(\gamma v\right),t\right)|dv|.

Proposition 5.19
.

For
a\geq 2q
,

\displaystyle\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}I_{Q(s)}(x,t,\gamma)\right]^{(a,0)}=

\displaystyle(st)^{-q}\frac{(4\pi)^{-\frac{q}{2}}}{\operatorname{det}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(1-\gamma|_{N^{\gamma}}\right)}\cdot\left[f_{0}df_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge df_{2q}\right]^{(2q,0)}

\displaystyle\wedge\left[\operatorname{det}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}/2}{\sinh\left(R^{\prime}/2\right)}\right)\operatorname{det}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1-\gamma|_{N^{\gamma}}e^{-R^{\prime\prime}}\right)\wedge\gamma^{V}e^{-F^{V}}\right]^{(a-2q,0)}+O(t^{-q+\frac{1}{2}}).

Proof.

By Theorem
A.22
,
[\gamma^{-1}I_{Q(s)}(x,t,\gamma)]^{(a,0)}
can be computed using
\left[\gamma^{-1}I_{P_{(2q)}(H_{R}+\partial_{t})^{-1}}(x,t,\gamma)\right]^{(a,0)}
. Thanks to Theorem
A.23
, we compute
\sigma\left[\gamma^{-1}I_{Q(s)}(x,t,\gamma)\right]^{(a,0)}
to be

\left[fdf_{1}\cdots df_{2q}\wedge\frac{(4\pi st)^{-\frac{a}{2}}}{\operatorname{det}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(1-\gamma|_{N^{\gamma}}\right)}\operatorname{det}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{stR^{\prime}/2}{\sinh\left(stR^{\prime}/2\right)}\right)\operatorname{det}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1-\gamma|_{N^{\gamma}}e^{-stR^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right]^{(a,0)}.

We observe that Theorem
A.23
is proved on compact manifolds. The same proof generalizes to prove this proposition as
f_{0}df_{1}\cdots df_{2q}
is of compact support.
∎

Set

\beta_{q}=\int_{1}^{2}\ldots\int_{1}^{2}(1+s_{1}+\cdots s_{q})^{-q}\;ds_{1}\ldots ds_{q}.

By Lemma
5.12
,
5.15
, and Proposition
5.19
, we conclude the following result:

Theorem 5.20
.

For the Type I terms in Definition
5.11
,

(5.21)

\lim_{t\o 0^{+}}\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm geo}_{s}(T_{I}(\sqrt{t}D))\\
=(-i)^{\frac{n}{2}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{a}{2}}\cdot\beta_{q}\cdot\left[f_{0}df_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge df_{2q}\right]^{(2q,0)}\wedge\left[\operatorname{det}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}/2}{\sinh\left(R^{\prime}/2\right)}\right)\operatorname{det}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1-\gamma|_{N^{\gamma}}e^{-R^{\prime\prime}}\right)\wedge\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma e^{-F^{V}})\right]^{(a-2q,0)}.

5.3.
Computation of type II

In this subsection, we will compute

\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm geo}_{s}\Big(T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\Big)\colon=\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\left(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{-}c(df_{1})B^{+}c(df_{2})\cdots C^{-}_{j}\cdots B^{+}c(df_{2q})\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right),

where
u(x)=\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-sx}ds=\frac{1-e^{-x}}{x}e^{-\frac{x}{2}}
and

\displaystyle C_{j}^{-}\colon=

\displaystyle t[u(tD^{2}),f_{j}]D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t\left[\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{C}u(t\lambda)(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\;d\lambda,f_{j}\right]D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{t}{2\pi i}\int_{C}u(t\lambda)\left[(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1},f_{j}\right]\;d\lambda\cdot D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{t}{2\pi i}\int_{C}u(t\lambda)(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\left[D^{2},f_{j}\right]D(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\;d\lambda.

Since
f_{0}
has compact support, the family

\lambda\mapsto f_{0}B^{-}c(df_{1})B^{+}c(df_{2})\cdots u(t\lambda)(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\left[D^{2},f_{j}\right]D(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}B^{+}c(df_{j+1})\ldots B^{+}c(df_{2q})e^{-tD^{2}}

is an integrable family of trace class operators on
C
. Hence, we can compute the trace of
T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)
by switching the order between trace and the contour integral, i.e.

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{\rm geo}\Big(T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\Big)=-\frac{t}{2\pi i}\int_{C}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\Big(\gamma^{-1}f_{0}B^{-}c(df_{1})B^{+}c(df_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\cdots u(t\lambda)(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\left[D^{2},f_{j}\right]D(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}B^{+}c(df_{j+1})\ldots B^{+}c(df_{2q})\left(e^{-tD^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\Big).

We observe that
B^{\pm}
are Volterra-related and
(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}
is a Volterra operator. As the argument in Lemma
5.12
, using Theorem
4.32
, we can compute the leading order term of
T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)
as follows,

(5.22)

\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{\rm geo}\big(T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\Big)=-\frac{t}{2\pi i}\int_{C}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{{\rm geo}}\Bigg[\gamma^{-1}B^{-}B^{+}\cdots u(t\lambda)(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\\
\left(f_{0}c(df_{1})\ldots c(df_{j-1})\left([D^{2},f_{j}]D\right)c(df_{j+1})\ldots c(df_{2q})(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}B^{+}\ldots(B^{+})^{3}\right)^{\gamma}\Bigg]+O(t).

In the above equation, we have used the identity that
e^{-tD^{2}}=(B^{+})^{2}
. Notice that the operators

(5.23)

\displaystyle e^{-tD^{2}}B^{-}B^{+}\cdots u(\lambda)(\lambda+tD^{2})^{-1}\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ (\lambda+tD^{2})^{-1}B^{+}\ldots(B^{+})^{3}

are both
\Gamma
-invariant but

(5.24)

\displaystyle f_{0}c(df_{1})\ldots c(df_{j-1})\left([D^{2},f_{j}]D\right)c(df_{j+1})\ldots c(df_{2q})

is not. Nevertheless, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.25
.

Suppose that
K_{1},K_{2}
are two
\Gamma
invariant smoothing operators on
M
. If
P
is a differential operator with compact support (in particular,
P
may not be
\Gamma
invariant), then

(5.26)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}^{{\rm geo}}(\gamma^{-1}K_{1}PK_{2}^{\gamma})=\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm geo}(\gamma^{-1}PK_{2}K_{1}^{\gamma}).

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm geo}(\gamma^{-1}K_{1}PK_{2}^{\gamma})=

\displaystyle\int_{M\times M}\operatorname{tr}\big(\gamma^{-1}k_{1}(x,y)p(y)k_{2}(y,\gamma x)\big)\;dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M\times M}\operatorname{tr}\big(k_{1}(x,\gamma y)\gamma^{-1}p(y)k_{2}(y,x)\big)\;dxdy=\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm geo}(PK_{2}K_{1}^{\gamma}).

In the second equality, we have applied the change of variable
x\mapsto\gamma^{-1}x
, and also the
\gamma
-invariance of the kernel
k_{1}
, i.e.
\gamma^{-1}k_{1}(\gamma^{-1}x,y)\gamma=k_{1}(x,\gamma y)
.
∎

Apply the above lemma to (
5.22
), we see that

(5.27)

\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm geo}_{s}\left(T_{II,j}(\sqrt{t}D)\right)=-\frac{t}{2\pi i}\int_{C}\operatorname{Tr}_{s}^{\rm geo}\Bigg[\gamma^{-1}f_{0}c(df_{1})\ldots c(df_{j-1})\left([D^{2},f_{j}]D\right)c(df_{j+1})\ldots c(df_{2q})\\
\left((\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}B^{q}u(t\lambda)(\lambda+D^{2})^{-1}\right)^{\gamma}\Bigg]\;d\lambda+O(t),
